## CP113 — 2026-09-30 18:32 +08:00 — table-aware native numbering discovery closes the last az6 boundary failure; az7 body/table × tracking × format/fault/Undo/Redo/reopen matrix passes

Frozen-R3 az7 SHA25680684903E7C4DF6ED10A0A3D0F91F68D4FA959D3E6B6CE126F7D0E6512469612 contains the post-az6 structural Find fix. The az6 failure was reproduced three times and is now explained by a Word 2021 search rule rather than by a bad native number: starting a Find from inside a table can snap the hit backward into an earlier cell even with wdFindStop. Logs show cursor22 returning the original match13:14 inside table range8:21. Character-wise cursor bumps therefore cannot prove progress and can either loop or skip later cells.

The root fix centralizes structural search-unit ownership in WordNumberingFieldCollection.CaptureSearchUnit. Body hits remain paragraph bounded. A hit inside a table is promoted to that containing Table.Range, so every relevant local field/OMath in the table is inspected once and the cursor advances past the complete structural unit. WordNativeNumberingAdoptionPlan and WordNativeOmmlFieldRefresh both consume the same helper; there is no table-specific label exception, no wdWithInTable layout query, no document.Fields fallback and no arbitrary +1 retry. Held R4 was not changed.

Actual word-native-number-adoption acceptance bound byte-for-byte to az7 now exits0. All four ownership contexts pass: body-plain, body-tracked, table-plain and table-tracked. Each context passes heading1-dot, continuous and heading1-dash exact native field replacement, physical Undo/Redo, terminal injected-fault rollback and save/reopen. progress.json records TerminalFaultRollback=true, Formats=3, UndoRedo=true, SaveReopen=true for all4. User default-number-format preference remains heading1-dot before/after in both registry views. The prior az6 failures are retained as negative evidence and are not relabeled.

This closes the specifically observed table search-unit defect, but az7 is not yet a release candidate. Next rerun the shared8/8 table ownership,7/7 actual Ribbon conversion, native numbering/revision suite, Core baseline, MathType create/fault and the exact large 1(1) continuation/update path on az7. An isolated Track Changes probe proves Word keeps the replaced predecessor field in the SAME `Range.Fields` collection: before replacement the number has STYLEREF+SEQ; after typed Fields.Add the live inserted SEQ is followed by a deleted predecessor SEQ whose Code/Result are revision-deleted and whose Code.Text is null. Az7 treated raw `Fields.Count/index` as current topology, so later native-format passes could choose a historical field span and then correctly fail the owner check. The root contract is therefore revision projection: all native/MathType numbering discovery must consume only current/inserted fields and ignore delete/move-from/conflict-delete predecessors. `WordNumberingFieldCollection.IsCurrentField` is now the single predicate used by document-wide discovery and the native adoption/refresh paths; mixed native tracked format churn currently passes all five formats (heading1 dot/dash, heading2 dot/dash, continuous) in the owned `word-native-mixed-number-format` acceptance. This is a topology-level fix, not an exception-message bypass.

A second numbering defect is independently reproduced before its fix by the new owned `word-native-edit-add-number` regression: one genuine field-free native `#(1.1-1)` predecessor plus a later unnumbered OMML edited to enable numbering yields duplicate `1.1-1` instead of `1.1-2`. Direct native creation already goes through `WordNativeNumberingAdoptionPlan`, but OMML edit/toggle still enters the older rebuild/attach path after source deletion, so the predecessor never becomes a real SEQ participant. This explains why “direct insert” and “redraw to OMML then edit/add number” can diverge even under the same visible format. The next product change must route edit-add-number through the same read-before-write adoption plan and document-wide format authority as native creation; do not add a special case for this fixture.

Both MathType screenshots are also reproduced below Word/OLE. For `100%\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi}+1=0`, MTEF writes the percent as a Text CHAR and the following upright `e` script base as another Text CHAR; because the writer emitted a one-character script base bare, MathType coalesced the adjacent CHARs into one native Text object `%e`, so the superscript attached to that merged object. This is why the earlier grouped-token percent escaping fix could pass `100%+2` yet still fail a real suffix beginning with a scripted base. The structural fix is to materialize every explicit MathML trailing-script base as one native LINE object; the exact probe then round-trips the original semantic signature and produces `100\text{\%}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\pi}+1=0`. For `\boldsymbol{\ddot{\eta}}`, the single-character embellishment fast path bypassed the canonical identifier/font-style emitter and called `ResolveTokenTypeface` directly; `bold-italic` therefore collapsed to built-in Vector/bold before the double-dot EMBELL was attached. Reusing the normal `EmitIdentifier` path while attaching EMBELL preserves the explicit Times New Roman bold-italic style and the exact semantic signature. Direct codec probes now pass both screenshot formulas. Full product/Word regression is still pending.

## CP130 — 2026-10-01 03:40 +08:00 — tracked-result field discovery root fixed without document.Fields; native literal lifecycle16/16 closes ordinary Update/reference/Undo/reopen

A targeted diagnostic proved a Word 2021 rule that was distinct from the literal m:nor transport itself. With Track Changes enabled, a direct REF whose field code is current but whose RESULT contains insertion/property/deletion revisions remains visible through document.Fields and through its exact paragraph.Fields, yet Word Find("REF"), Find("^d REF"), Find("^d") and Find("^19") all skip that paragraph. The failing owned fixture showed paragraph4 range144:207 owning exactly one current REF while every Find route stopped after the preceding SEQ paragraph. Therefore the previous bounded field collector had a structural blind spot, not an IsCurrentField error.

WordNumberingFieldCollection now keeps its fast Find-based body/table discovery and adds a second bounded entrance only for actual revision-owned structural units: enumerate document.Revisions, promote each revision range to its exact paragraph/table search unit, deduplicate by unit start, then inspect only that unit's local Fields and project through IsCurrentField. It does NOT restore document.Fields or scan every paragraph. WordNativeOmmlFieldRefresh reference capture now consumes this shared field collector instead of maintaining a separate REF-Find implementation.

The previously failing body-True-h1-dash literal lifecycle case passes after this change. The complete word-native-literal-lifecycle matrix then passes16/16: body/table × Track Changes off/on × heading1/heading2 × dot/dash. ## CP227 — 2026-10-03 — Named spacing authority bounded; independent clipboard issue scoped out

Read-only tracing establishes the remaining native-spacing loss without new native runs. Production MathJax maps thin/medium/thick/negative-thin commands to3/18,4/18,5/18,-3/18, then BaseMethods.Spacer uses em() rounded to three decimals. Backend inputs are therefore0.167em,0.222em,0.278em,-0.167em. Current unsupported-width EmitMathSpace maps all four to fnSPACE ASCII CHAR0200982000 and the reader returns0.2em. MathSpaceContract's exact decimal -> hspace recovery is not the source of this loss; missing native signed-advance projection is.

Prior native controls show EF02 minus EF01 is72/384=0.1875em in the measured context, not exact1/6; EF22 minus EF01 advances+12 units rather than negative. EQN_PREFS nonoperator spacing100% versus0% leaves the measured origins and corresponding ink hashes unchanged. No-space deltas include boundary layout and are not nominal encoded widths; the measurements do not establish universal preference independence. Vendor TeX base rules identify nominal space codes, but Texvc explicitly states native thick1/3 is not exact TeX5/18; Presentation MathML input line737 delegates to opaque space{width}, while Texvc input ignores hspace. Inspected public MTEF CHAR records have no width field and the preference list has no independent thin/medium/thick parameter. NUDGE specifies object offset, not a proved advance contract; neither exact kern support nor universally visual-only behavior is inferred. No approximation aliases, guessed negative-space mapping, nudge encoding, rejection layer or hidden geometry substitute is added. This limits the available documented encoder, not a blanket assertion that MathType cannot represent arbitrary spacing.

Evidence reused: native-spacing-authority-readonly-20261002T123100Z.json, spacing-upright-result/spacing-measurements.json, spacing-nonoperator0-result/spacing-measurements.json and their original prepared payloads. A reliable future vendor-import output could establish the missing binary advance/quantization contract. Existing1em/2em and source exact-dimension tests were not rerun.

The three known extreme-decimal latex-expanded losses occur specifically in MathEditor.tsx handleMultiLineCopy lines7389–7415, which writes clipboard formats. This is an independent multiline-copy feature, not the current six-way conversion or durable source recovery path, so it is recorded but not modified in this task. Next read-only fidelity review targets the already-vendor-documented remaining subset/superset alternate tuples in the existing shared glyph registry; no production mapping is assumed before checking current loss and exact ordinary/alternate authority.

## CP230 — 2026-10-03 — Fixed-run delimiter recovery evidence and access boundary

Read-only classification of C01131, C01434/C01435 and C01474/C01475 confirms their parentheses are ordinary native m:r/m:t; the CP222 scalable controls use m:d. OMML run properties have no growth property, whereas delimiter growth belongs to m:dPr/m:grow. A possible shared reverse-projection correction is to preserve fixed glyph geometry with stretchy=false only when the emitted delimiter mo is proven to originate from an ordinary math run. It must retain role/style/text/script ownership; fence=false is not justified, because a fixed glyph can still have fence role. Existing comparer behavior already respects explicit fixed geometry.

This is NOT implemented. Devspace read of the installed Office OMML2MML.XSL was denied as outside allowed roots. No shell or alternate-path read was attempted to bypass that denial, and no in-repo XSL copy was found. The exact vendor tokenizer emission context is therefore not inspected. A global mo annotation or a test based merely on absence of an m:d ancestor would be unjustified: fixed runs can occur inside real delimiter objects, and nary/accent/group-character operators are generated elsewhere. Positive and negative coverage must include native plain/scripted/merged runs, genuine m:d with both grow settings, one-sided/mixed endpoints, nested structures, normal-text prose, repeated identical script slots and operator emulators. Official schema references: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.math.runproperties?view=openxml-3.0.1 and https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.math.growoperators?view=openxml-3.0.1 . These establish structural evidence, not visual acceptance or original author intent. Next read-only candidate is the separately reproduced monospace projection loss C01293; no new production change is inferred from this checkpoint.

## CP231 — 2026-10-03 — Mathematical monospace mapping and digit default conflict reproduced

The native standard has a mathematical monospace representation: m:rPr/m:scr=monospace with m:sty=p, without m:nor or font overrides. Source: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/documentformat.openxml.math.script?view=openxml-2.20.0 . The first direct managed baseline has17 cases:8 failed,9 passed (cp231-monospace/cp231-monospace-before.trx). Forward Latin/digit monospace produces empty rPr, including C01293. Reverse native monospace x/AZ/az already works, but native monospace09 returns plain mn because the existing upright-digit default rule suppresses its non-Roman script. Ordinary p/i/b/bi digit controls pass. Thus the evidence supports a narrow forward native-alphabet mapping and correction of the conflicting reverse digit default, not a new font fallback or conversion to prose. ASCII mathematical monospace letters/digits are the supported repertoire under test; Greek/punctuation are not declared covered merely because the style string exists. Four owned Word probes (Latin, digits, mixed ordinary/monospace and scripts) have been prepared but not yet run. Implementation and final validation were pending at this initial entry.

CP231 final: the app-owned forward CreateMathScrStyProp adaptation now emits the standard monospace script/plain-style pair. Reverse upright-digit default is limited to absent/roman script using string($scr), so non-Roman mathematical alphabets are no longer erased. Latin reverse mapping already worked and was not rewritten. The first adjacent run exposed an XPath empty-node-set comparison regression (redundant normal attribute on ordinary plain digits); that failed evidence was retained and the shared default condition corrected. Final21 dedicated and100 focused/adjacent tests pass, including ordinary p/i/b/bi digits, representable sans-serif/double-struck digit controls, inherited/local overrides, mixed roles and script ownership. Full managed aggregate2184 passed,0 failed,0 skipped with the same four native exclusions; this incremental run emitted no compiler/analyzer warnings. Evidence cp231-monospace/cp231-monospace-final.trx and shared-contract-aggregate/cp231-final-integrated.trx.
